{"parentUuid":"85df1d72-610f-46cd-927a-7571f3cc5020","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a5d34dd62f848ca0a","attachment":{"type":"skill_listing","content":"- artifact-planner: Produce a self-contained, navigable HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPLIT STORYBOARD: each scene pairs a rich UI/mockup (left) with a before→after Mermaid diagram of the structural change (right), plus a \"what this lets you do\" line and an \"under the hood\" strip. Embeds a machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, walk through, or visualize any multi-step flow: onboarding, checkout, auth, OAuth integrations, wizards, subscription cancellation, push notifications, support ticket lifecycles, AI chat execution, CI/CD pipelines, refund flows, admin features, migrations, webhook lifecycles, or anything else with sequenced stages and decision points. Trigger on \"create an artifact for…\", \"plan this flow\", \"make a flow plan\", \"visualize this flow\", \"show me the stages of…\", \"mock up the flow for…\", \"I want to plan X visually\", \"build a walkthrough of…\", or any time the user has just described a feature/flow and would benefit from a single-page visual + structured spec they can revisit, share, or drop back into a new chat. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- artifact-planner-canvas: Produce a self-contained HTML artifact that visualizes a multi-stage flow or feature plan as a SPATIAL CANVAS: the WHOLE plan on one pannable/zoomable Figma-style board — frontend mockups in a top band, each stage's before→after Mermaid diagram directly beneath (dashed spine connectors), branches as tinted lanes with fork/rejoin curves, decisions and open questions as sticky notes placed next to the stages they affect, and double-click-anywhere comment stickies. This is the CANVAS variant of `artifact-planner` (which pages one scene at a time as a split storyboard): prefer the canvas when the user wants to see everything at once, compare stages spatially, or leave free-floating comments; prefer the paged storyboard for linear walkthroughs and presentations. Embeds the same machine-readable plan spec so a brand-new session can re-import the artifact and keep iterating. Works for ANY project — discover the target repo's stack/URLs/conventions at runtime rather than assuming them. Use whenever the user wants to plan, mock up, or visualize any multi-step flow (onboarding, checkout, auth, wizards, CI/CD pipelines, migrations, webhook lifecycles…) AND signals a canvas: \"canvas artifact\", \"spatial plan\", \"put the plan on a canvas/board\", \"Figma-style board\", \"show me the whole flow at once\", \"map it out spatially\" — or when they ask for the canvas version of an existing artifact-plan. Each artifact is one HTML file at `/tmp/<slug>.html`, opens in Chrome, and embeds its own plan JSON.\n- auto-rough-cut: Turn a folder of raw class-video recordings — separately recorded pristine voice tracks (Audacity .wav) plus their screen/camera videos (.mp4) — into a draggable Adobe Premiere Pro FCP7 XML that is silence-cut to Ray's Recut preferences, has the pristine audio synced and linked to the video, transcribes every kept chunk, and flags repeated takes / filler / breaths in PURPLE for a fast review pass. Use this skill whenever Ray points at a folder of recording pairs and wants a rough cut, first assembly, or \"silence cut\", or says things like \"cut the silences\", \"sync my audio and video and remove the dead air\", \"make a Premiere XML from these recordings\", \"apply my Recut process / record preferences\", \"flag the retakes\", \"auto rough-cut this video\", \"turn my Audacity audio + screen recording into a timeline\", or stages new recordings under a \"[Class Videos]\" / to-film folder. Trigger it even if Recut, transcription, or retakes aren't all named — any \"remove the silence and give me a Premiere XML I can still drag around\" request for paired audio+video is this skill. Distinct from recut-xml-fixer (which only repairs an XML Recut already exported); this skill does the whole cut from raw media.\n- auto-spec: Turn a feature brief (inline text or a stub plan file) into an implementation-ready plan via an autonomous two-agent interview, an external-API research verification pass, and a two-agent codebase-grounded planning pass — and, in its bootstrap mode, build/refresh the personalized digital twin that powers that interview by mining your Claude Code conversation history. Use when the user says \"auto spec\", \"spec autopilot\", \"auto interview\", \"flesh out this plan\", \"/auto-spec <file or brief>\", or otherwise wants a plan written without manually answering questions; ALSO use for \"create my auto-spec\", \"clone my brain\", \"make a spec twin\", \"personalize auto-spec\", \"build my decision profile\", or any request to analyze conversation history for decision patterns. Produces a single plan file with Feature Brief, Spec Decisions, External Research, Codebase Map, and phased Implementation Plan — ready to hand to `/implement-task` — or, in bootstrap mode, a refreshed `decision_profile.md` + `qa_examples.json` digital twin.\n- chronicle\n- codex-consult: OpenAI Codex CLI wrapper — three modes. Code review: independent diff review via\ncodex review with pass/fail gate. Challenge: adversarial mode that tries to break\nyour code. Consult: ask codex anything with session continuity for follow-ups.\nThe \"200 IQ autistic developer\" second opinion. Use when asked to \"codex review\",\n\"codex challenge\", \"ask codex\", \"second opinion\", or \"consult codex\".\n- improve-claude-md: improve a CLAUDE.md file using <important if> blocks to improve instruction adherence\n- interview: Relentlessly interrogate a plan, decision, or idea one question at a time until the user and Claude reach a genuinely shared understanding. Use this whenever the user wants to stress-test their thinking, pressure-test a plan, be challenged on an idea, or says any of \"interview me\", \"grill me\", \"grill this\", \"poke holes in this\", \"stress-test this\", \"ask me everything\", \"interrogate me\", \"what am I missing\", or \"/interview\". Also reach for it when the user drops a half-formed plan and asks for questions before you build anything, or when a request is underspecified enough that guessing would waste real work. Do not write code, files, or plans while this skill is active until the user says the picture is complete.\n- slackbot-message: Send Slack messages to a named channel via a bot user. Use this skill whenever you need to notify Ray on Slack, post a status update, alert a channel, deliver a short summary, or when he asks you to \"post to slack\", \"message #channel\", \"send a slack message\", \"ping slack\", \"slack me\", \"let me know on slack\", or names a specific Slack channel by name. Also use it after long-running tasks finish if Ray asked for Slack notifications. The bot is invited to the relevant channels, so the script can post to any channel by its human-readable name (no need to know channel IDs).\n- spec-developer-v2: Interview the user about a rough idea or brief with sharp, non-obvious product and technical questions, then write a concise implementation-ready spec. Use when the user wants to spec something out, develop a vague idea into requirements, or turn a product concept into a detailed plan.\n- sync-skills: Commit and push GLOBAL skill changes to the personal skills git repo\n(github.com/ray-amjad/private-skills). Auto-detects what changed in\n~/.claude/skills/, stages the changes, generates a meaningful commit message,\nand pushes to origin main. Use this skill whenever you finish creating or\nediting a global skill, or when the user says \"sync skills\", \"push skills\",\n\"save skills\", \"/sync-skills\", or asks to commit skill changes to git.\nIMPORTANT: Only trigger this skill when working at the project level (i.e., when\nthe working directory is a project repo, not from within ~/.claude/skills/ itself).\nDo NOT use this for project-scoped skills (e.g. skills under a project's own\n.claude/skills/, like ray-os's sentence-mining) — those are committed to that\nproject's repo, not here.\n- ultragoal\n- vercel-logs: Check Vercel production logs, errors, and build output. Use this skill whenever you need to\ndiagnose a deployment error, check for runtime errors, investigate 500s, or the user asks\n\"check the logs\", \"what's the error\", \"why is it broken\", \"check vercel logs\", or any\nrequest to look at Vercel deployment/runtime/build logs. Also trigger on `/vercel-logs`.\n\nCRITICAL: Also invoke this skill BEFORE running any `vercel logs` command yourself —\neven when no one asked you to. The CLI has non-obvious flag interactions (implicit\nfollow mode, stderr-only output, --since rejection) that cause hangs or silent failures\nif you guess at flags. If you're about to type `vercel logs` in a Bash call, load this\nskill first.\n- vercel-monitor: Watch a Vercel deployment in the background and notify when it reaches Ready/Error/Canceled.\nUse this skill whenever the user pushes to main, runs `git push`, deploys to Vercel, or asks\nto \"monitor the deploy\", \"watch the build\", \"check if it deployed\", or anything similar —\neven if they don't explicitly say \"Vercel\". Also trigger on `/vercel-monitor`. This replaces\nolder patterns of spawning a haiku subagent or polling via the Vercel MCP, both of which\nwaste tokens and context on large projects.\n- anki-cleanup\n- binary-explorer: Reverse-engineer any desktop application OR Claude Code's own binary to understand how it works behind the scenes — the APIs it calls, the internal flow, the models/providers it uses, its feature flags, config constants, embedded prompts, IPC protocols. For general apps it works on Electron apps (Wispr Flow, Slack, Discord, VS Code, Cursor, Notion, Linear…), native macOS .app bundles, single-executable bundles (Bun/Node/Deno/Go SEA), and raw Mach-O / ELF / PE binaries. It ALSO reverse-engineers Claude Code's own binary — it knows Claude Code's mangled variable conventions, feature-flag patterns, and embedded-prompt layout. Use this skill whenever the user points at a desktop app and wants to know \"how does X work under the hood\", \"what API does it call\", \"how does it do Y\", \"what's the flow for Z\", \"reverse engineer this app\", \"figure out how this feature works\", \"what endpoints does it hit\", \"does it run locally or in the cloud\", \"what model does it use\", or names a specific app path (.app, .exe, a binary). ALSO use it whenever the user asks about a Claude Code feature, feature flag, hidden toggle, internal prompt, or wants to understand how something in Claude Code works under the hood — especially unreleased, gated, or undocumented functionality — or says \"how does X work in the binary\", \"what does this feature flag do\", \"find the prompt for X\", \"reverse engineer X\", or mentions a specific codename/flag. Also trigger when the user wants to audit an app's network behavior, understand its architecture, or ext…\n- class-scriptwriter: Turn a raw idea, source tweet, video transcript, feature announcement, or rough outline into a polished video script for Ray's Agentic Coding School classes. Use this skill whenever the user says \"write a class script\", \"turn this into a class video\", \"script this for the [business/claude-code/prompt-engineering/skills/context-engineering/techniques/claude-chat/claude-cowork/correction] class\", \"draft an agentic coding school video\", or whenever the user is staging content in `/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os/projects/agentic-coding-school/to-film/` and wants it written up. Default to this skill for any new file destined for the `to-film/` tree, even if the user does not name it explicitly. Produces an essay-style markdown script with the correct frontmatter, horizontal rules between sections, inline image embed placeholders, and a Demo section, written in Ray's direct second-person voice.\n- content-cascade\n- discord-archive\n- excalidraw-bundle: Bundle a set of section images into a single .excalidraw file laid out as a grid — one column per section in document order, the section's variations stacked vertically — and save it to ~/Downloads, ready to drag into excalidraw.com or the Obsidian Excalidraw plugin.\n- excalidraw-codex: Generate excalidraw-style explanation images using the Codex CLI (codex exec --json) instead of a paid API. Use when the user wants visual explanations, diagrams, or illustrations in the hand-drawn excalidraw aesthetic and asks for the codex path. Codex's bundled image_gen tool runs through the logged-in account, so no GEMINI_API_KEY is required and it's often faster than the Gemini route. Triggers on \"generate an excalidraw image\", \"excalidraw-codex\", \"use codex for excalidraw\", or any request for excalidraw-style diagrams where no engine is named. This is the RECOMMENDED DEFAULT path for excalidraw image generation: it needs no API key and runs through the logged-in Codex account. Sibling skill [[excalidraw-gemini]] does the same job through the paid Gemini API. Default to codex whenever the user does not explicitly ask for the Gemini route.\n- excalidraw-codex-simple\n- excalidraw-deck: Build hand-drawn, Excalidraw-style HTML slide decks that explain a concept through sketch-aesthetic vector diagrams — rough.js strokes on a dark canvas, full-bleed, one diagram per slide with a caption underneath, arrow-key and tap navigation. Use this skill whenever the user wants explainer slides, a concept deck, diagram slides, or a talk/video presentation in the hand-drawn sketch look, or says things like \"make slides for X\", \"build a deck for X\", \"add a slide in this style\", \"turn this into a sketch/excalidraw-style deck\", \"another slide like the workflow deck\", or points at an existing rough.js deck and wants more in the same style. This is for self-contained vector-diagram decks — NOT the Claude Code terminal-aesthetic slides (use cc-terminal-slides) and NOT raster image generation (use excalidraw-gen, which calls an image model). Reach for this skill proactively for any \"explain this concept on slides\" request, even when the user does not say the word \"excalidraw\".\n- extract-wisdom: Extract structured wisdom, insights, and actionable takeaways from any content — podcasts, articles, videos, transcripts, books, or conversations. Use this skill whenever the user asks to \"extract wisdom\", \"summarize insights\", \"pull out key ideas\", \"what are the takeaways\", \"extract the best parts\", \"distill this content\", \"what did I learn from this\", or provides a transcript/article and asks for a structured breakdown. Also trigger when the user pastes or references long-form content and wants the signal extracted from the noise — even if they don't use the word \"wisdom\" specifically. Inspired by Daniel Miessler's Fabric pattern. Has a BATCH MODE for running this extraction across a whole YouTube channel in bulk: lists videos with yt-dlp, filters by duration, downloads transcripts, then fans out one extract-wisdom subagent per video in batches of 5, writing one wisdom file per video to disk. Trigger batch mode whenever the user wants wisdom/insights/takeaways from a YouTube channel in bulk: \"extract wisdom from this channel\", \"go through @<handle>'s long videos\", \"summarise everything on <channel>\", \"give me the best ideas from <creator>'s podcast\", \"process a whole channel into wisdom notes\", or \"I want to read the highlights from every video on @<handle> longer than 20 minutes\".\n- humanizer\n- idea-foundry\n- image-cleanup: Clean up unreferenced images in Obsidian vault folders. Deletes unused images, renames excalidraw files with descriptive names based on image content (using vision), and moves everything to vault root images/ folder. Use after picking favorites from excalidraw-gen batches. Triggers on \"clean up images\", \"delete unused images\", \"tidy up images\", \"image cleanup\", or \"/image-cleanup\".\n- linkedin: All LinkedIn tasks — writing posts (text or carousel), scouting sources to post about, checking post performance, browsing the feed, or analyzing competitors. Triggers on any mention of LinkedIn, including \"write a LinkedIn post\", \"make a LinkedIn carousel\", \"swipeable LinkedIn post\", \"LinkedIn slides\", \"check my LinkedIn\", \"how did my posts do\", \"LinkedIn competitors\", \"post on LinkedIn\", \"browse LinkedIn feed\". Also triggers on \"check post engagement\", \"update post metrics\", or reviewing social media performance. Also use it for finding something to post about — \"find me a LinkedIn topic\", \"run a source sweep\", \"scout me something on MCP\", \"what should I post today\", or reviewing the drafts the daily scout left in Slack\n- newsletter-writer: Write personal newsletters in a conversational, essay-style format inspired by Ali Abdaal's style. Use when the user wants to write a newsletter issue, draft an email to their list, write a LifeNotes-style essay, or asks for help with newsletter writing. Triggers on requests like \"write a newsletter\", \"draft a newsletter about...\", \"help me write my weekly email\", \"newsletter style\", or when the user provides a topic and wants it turned into a newsletter format.\n- rakuten-hotels\n- recut-xml-fixer: Reshape an FCP7 XML exported from a silence-cutting app (Recut, AutoEdit, Vika, etc.) so Adobe Premiere Pro imports it cleanly with the user's pristine external audio (e.g. Audio.wav from Audacity) linked to the video instead of the camera/screen-recording's own audio. Use this skill whenever the user points at an .xml or sequence file from Recut, AutoEdit, or any auto-cutter and wants to swap in their own audio track, drop the redundant on-camera audio, fix vertical alignment between video and audio cuts, or fix a Premiere \"import failed\" error on a Recut-style XML. Triggers on phrases like \"fix this Recut XML\", \"swap the audio in this premiere XML\", \"align the cuts to my Audio.wav\", \"remove the loom audio track\", \"merge V1 and A1 in this XML so they're linked\", or any reference to a workflow combining a silence-cutter app's XML with a separately recorded pristine audio file.\n- sentence-mining: Build and maintain Japanese sentence-mining cards for Anki, fully self-contained (no AnkiMorphs install required) and configurable per user via a one-time `/sentence-mining setup`. Six modes, and the skill ASKS which one you want on every launch. (1) Video mode — paste any Instagram reel, YouTube video/Short, TikTok, Twitter video, or local file → yt-dlp + AssemblyAI + SudachiPy + a built-in i+1 known-word diff produces draft cards. (2) Bank mode — give a list of target words → find the best example sentence for each through one canonical cascade (Immersion Kit → Nadeshiko → sentencesearch → kotu → your locally-indexed subs2srs .apkg banks), re-ranked by your own i+1, with native audio and a screenshot where the source ships one. (3) Replace mode — fix existing cards whose example sentence is bad (too short, a fragment, incomprehensible): pull a better sentence through the same cascade, edit the card in place (archiving the old sentence to a previous_versions field), and rehabilitate the card so you re-learn it fresh. (4) Leech mode — work the cards Anki flagged as leeches, but filtered by whether they are STILL failing: `tag:leech` is a scar Anki never removes, so most of a leech list is usually cards that recovered months ago (43 of Ray's 78 sat at 21-338 day intervals). Cards below the interval threshold get a fresh sentence through the cascade plus full rehabilitation (reps/lapses zeroed, due today); recovered cards just have the tag stripped with their scheduling untouched; words rarer than a JPDB frequen…\n- transcribe-youtube-video: Transcribe audio or video into a clean .txt + timestamped .srt + YouTube chapter timestamps. Re-encodes any WAV/MP4 to a small MP3 before upload, mines on-screen text with OCR to seed AssemblyAI key terms, runs AssemblyAI Universal-3 Pro speech-to-text, then generates punchy chapter markers from the SRT. Use whenever the user wants to transcribe a video or audio file, generate subtitles/captions or an SRT, create a YouTube transcript or chapters, or convert a recording to text. Also trigger when the user has a WAV/MP4/MOV and wants text out of it, even without saying 'transcribe'.\n- ungate-claude-code-feature: Enable a gated, experimental, or staged-rollout Claude Code feature on your own machine by finding its GrowthBook feature flag in the binary and force-enabling it through a Proxyman interception rule (with an optional disk-cache fast-path). Use this skill whenever the user wants to ungate, unlock, force-enable, or \"turn on\" a Claude Code feature hidden behind a flag — phrasings like \"ungate the X feature\", \"enable ultrareview\", \"unlock [feature name]\", \"turn on [feature]\", \"enable the workflow/agent tool\", \"this feature won't show up even with the env var\", \"force-enable a tengu_ flag\", \"flip the flag for X\", \"intercept the feature flag\", \"unlock a beta Claude Code feature\", or any case where a Claude Code feature is gated and the user wants it on. Also trigger when the user references a specific tengu_ flag name, or the existing \"GrowthBook Flag Enabler\" Proxyman rule, or wants to add another flag to it. For the user's own Claude Code install and account.\n- watch-next-jp\n- wisdom-to-acs-gap: Turn ONE video (YouTube URL/id or a pasted transcript) into an Agentic Coding School (ACS) content-gap report: extract its full wisdom, promote the 1-3 load-bearing \"spine\" ideas, deep-dive each in prose, gap-check each against the live ACS catalog, and pitch the buildable new ACS videos. The analysis engine behind the acs/gap-scout routine, but also runs standalone. Use this skill WHENEVER the user pastes or links a coding/AI video (podcast, talk, tutorial, feature walkthrough) and wants the ACS angle: \"what ACS video could come from this\", \"run the gap scout on this\", \"gap-check this against the catalog\", \"find the spine idea\", \"is this already covered in the school\", \"what should I film from this\", \"mine this video for ACS\", or just drops a transcript/link and asks what's video-worthy. Also the per-video worker the gap-scout routine calls for each swept video. Do NOT use for a bulk channel sweep (that is the gap-scout routine / youtube-outlier-research routine) or for plain wisdom extraction with no ACS gap-check (that is extract-wisdom).\n- x-article\n- x-thread-miner\n- x-video-promo\n- youtube-ab-tester: End-to-end YouTube A/B testing for Ray's channel (@RAmjad) — title generation via subagent, thumbnail image generation via `gemini-3-pro-image-preview` at 1K, results recording, and the underlying scripts/golden references all live here. Use whenever the user wants to brainstorm titles, generate thumbnails (text or images), record A/B test results, analyze title/thumbnail performance, or asks things like \"suggest titles\", \"what titles should I test\", \"make a thumbnail\", \"thumbnail variations\", \"5 thumbnails for this video\", \"Matt style\", \"Nate style\", \"record these results\", \"what's working\", or \"update the A/B test results\". Also trigger when the user shares a screenshot of YouTube A/B test results and wants analysis or recording. Do NOT use for competitor title research (the youtube-outlier-research routine covers niche/competitor research) or video scripting (use youtube-scriptwriter instead).\n- youtube-miner\n- youtube-writer\n- review-plan\n- find-prompt-injections\n- loop-opportunities\n- loop-opportunities-personas\n- loop-opportunities-topdown\n- loopy-ai-class-review\n- loopy-ai-source-enrich\n- loopy-ai-stub-walk\n- mine-video-batch\n- claude-md-management:revise-claude-md\n- skill-creator:skill-creator: Create new skills, modify and improve existing skills, and measure skill performance. Use when users want to create a skill from scratch, edit, or optimize an existing skill, run evals to test a skill, benchmark skill performance with variance analysis, or optimize a skill's description for better triggering accuracy.\n- mermaid-diagram:mermaid-diagram-generator: Generate interactive HTML diagrams with Mermaid.js for visualizing flows, sequences, architectures, and processes. Use when the user asks to \"create a diagram\", \"visualize the flow\", \"draw a sequence diagram\", \"show me how X works\", or needs visual documentation of system interactions, workflows, database schemas, or technical processes.\n- frontend-design:frontend-design: Guidance for distinctive, intentional visual design when building new UI or reshaping an existing one. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- claude-md-management:claude-md-improver\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- claude-in-chrome: Automates your Chrome browser to interact with web pages - clicking elements, filling forms, capturing screenshots, reading console logs, and navigating sites. Opens pages in new tabs within your existing Chrome session. Requires site-level permissions before executing (configured in the extension). - When the user wants to interact with web pages, automate browser tasks, capture screenshots, read console logs, or perform any browser-based actions. Always invoke BEFORE attempting to use any mcp__claude-in-chrome__* tools.\n- run: Launch and drive this project's app to see a change working. Use when asked to run, start, or screenshot the app, or to confirm a change works in the real app (not just tests). First looks for a project skill that already covers launching the app; otherwise falls back to built-in patterns per project type (CLI, server, TUI, Electron, browser-driven, library).\n- init\n- review: Review a GitHub pull request; Findings below.\n2\t\n3\t### Candidate\n4\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/post-process.ts`\n5\tLine: 18\n6\tInvariant: I1\n7\tClaim: `ESTIMATED_POST_PROCESS_CREDITS = 1.0` is a flat constant independent of input size, while the route accepts `text` up to 100,000 chars (~25k prompt tokens) and bills the real token cost.\n8\tFailure scenario: A user with 1.0 credit POSTs 100,000 chars of text with the default provider (`cerebras`, $0.35/M in, $0.75/M out). The preflight passes; the corrected text comes back; `deductCredits` bills ~$0.0275 = ~28 credits. `deductCreditBalance` floors the balance at 0 (`nextjs/app/api/license/credits/route.ts:112`), so HyperWhisper eats ~27 credits of provider spend. With `X-LLM-Provider: grok` ($1.25/$2.50 per M) the same request bills ~94 credits against a 1.0 reservation. Because `deductCredits()` is fire-and-forget, nothing upstream ever learns the deduction failed — the SIGTERM drain is defeated too (it awaits a promise that resolves \"successfully\" having charged nothing). At scale this is uncapped free usage during every `nextjs` incident window.\n7\tConfidence: high\n8\t\n9\t### Candidate\n10\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/transcribe.ts`\n11\tLine: 678\n12\tInvariant: I2\n13\tClaim: When any STT adapter returns `source: 'no_speech'`, `deductCredits()` is skipped entirely (`if (!noSpeech)`), yet that value is returned **after** a completed, provider-billed transcription — all 11 adapters produce it from a successful 200/`status: completed` response (`deepgram.ts:168`, `groq.ts:129`, `elevenlabs.ts:202`, `openai.ts:132`, `mistral.ts:132`, `assemblyai.ts:301` and `:548`, `soniox.ts:289`, `xai-stt.ts:152`, `azure-mai.ts:212`, `gemini.ts:264`, `google-chirp.ts:269`).\n14\tFailure scenario: Deepgram/Groq/OpenAI/AssemblyAI/ElevenLabs all meter by audio duration submitted, not by transcript content. A user who uploads silence, room noise, music, or non-speech audio gets a fully paid-for transcription for 0 credits every single time. This is a deterministic, trivially repeatable free-transcription oracle: a client can push arbitrarily long silent WAVs and HyperWhisper eats 100% of the provider bill. Note the adapters also hardcode `durationSeconds: 0, costUsd: 0` on this path, so even removing the guard would still deduct nothing real.\n15\tConfidence: high\n16\t\n17\t### Candidate\n18\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/transcribe.ts`\n19\tLine: 550\n20\tInvariant: I2\n21\tClaim: A `ProviderUnavailableError` thrown *after* the upstream has already done (and billed) the work causes `continue` to the next chain member; only the surviving provider's `result.costUsd` is ever deducted at line 679. The first provider's real spend is never charged.\n22\tFailure scenario: Concrete triggers that all fire post-billing: `deepgram.ts:159` and `groq.ts:119` throw `ProviderUnavailableError('malformed 200 response body')` after a **200 OK** (Deepgram/Groq already transcribed and billed); `elevenlabs.ts:185` throws on a non-JSON 200; `assemblyai.ts:604` and `soniox.ts:263` throw `poll deadline exceeded after 240000ms` after the async job was created and is being processed (AssemblyAI/Soniox bill the submitted job regardless of whether we collect the result); `fetchWithTimeout`'s `kind: 'timeout'` is explicitly documented at `transcribe.ts:527` as \"we gave up; upstream may have been fine\". So a >4-minute audio file on AssemblyAI, or one edge-proxy hiccup on Deepgram, produces two paid provider calls and exactly one deduction — the user pays for the cheaper fallback while HyperWhisper absorbs the abandoned job.\n23\tConfidence: high\n24\t\n25\t### Candidate\n26\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/assistant.ts`\n27\tLine: 351\n28\tInvariant: I2\n29\tClaim: The deduction is gated on `if (costUsd > 0)`, and `streamAnthropicChat`'s `cancel()` handler (`providers/anthropic.ts:250-259`) resolves the cost from `inputTokens`/`outputTokens` that are only populated once Anthropic's `message_start` event has been parsed — so a client disconnect before that event yields `costUsd === 0` and no deduction at all.\n30\tFailure scenario: A client POSTs `/assistant` with a 10 MB screenshot plus conversation history and drops the connection within the first few hundred milliseconds (or a flaky mobile/desktop network does it for them). Anthropic has already received and is billing the full vision request — a screenshot is easily 1,500-3,000 input tokens plus the message history — but `costUsd` is 0, the `if` short-circuits, and the user is charged nothing. Repeating this in a loop is unmetered Anthropic vision spend against a valid account key that never drops below its balance, so `validateCredits` never blocks it either.\n31\tConfidence: medium\n32\t\n33\t### Candidate\n34\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/ws-streaming-deepgram.ts`\n35\tLine: 208\n36\tInvariant: I2\n37\tClaim: `endSession()` guards on `creditsUsed > 0`, but `creditsForCost()` (`lib/cost-calculator.ts:496-503`) returns a hard floor of `0.1` for **any** input including `costUsd <= 0` — so the guard is always true and a deduction fires on every session teardown, including sessions where zero audio was ever forwarded to Deepgram.\n38\tFailure scenario: `onOpen` returns early at line 241 when `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` is unset, closing the client socket with 1011; `onClose` then runs `endSession()` with `totalDurationSeconds === 0`, and the user is charged 0.1 credits for a server misconfiguration in which no upstream call was made at all. Same for a client that opens the socket and disconnects before speaking, or a Deepgram handshake that fails. A desktop client that opens a streaming socket on app launch or retries a failing connection burns 0.1 credits per attempt against a real balance, and the `session_complete` message it receives reports `credits_used: 0.1` for `duration_seconds: 0`. (Note `estimateCreditsForCost` at line 505 returns 0 for the same input — the two siblings disagree.)\n39\tConfidence: medium\n40\t\n41\t### Candidate\n42\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/ws-streaming-deepgram.ts`\n43\tLine: 188\n44\tInvariant: I2\n45\tClaim: A live streaming session accumulates `totalDurationSeconds` in memory and only converts it to a `deductCredits()` call at teardown; `drainPendingDeductions()` (`middleware/credits.ts:106`) drains `inFlightDeductions`, which a not-yet-ended session has not entered, and `index.ts:99` then calls `process.exit(0)` without ever invoking `endSession()`.\n46\tFailure scenario: On every Fly deploy or scale-down, each in-progress `/ws/streaming-deepgram` session is terminated with its entire accumulated duration unbilled — up to `MAX_SESSION_AUDIO_BYTES` (100 MB ≈ 52 minutes) of Deepgram Nova-3 audio that Deepgram has already metered and invoiced. The 4-second graceful-shutdown window explicitly exists to prevent exactly this loss for HTTP routes but does not cover the WebSocket route.\n47\tConfidence: medium\n48\t\n49\t### Candidate\n50\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/lib/utils.ts`\n51\tLine: 78\n52\tInvariant: I2\n53\tClaim: `retryWithBackoff` re-invokes `fn()` on **any** thrown error, and the LLM clients throw *after* a billed 200 — `cerebras.ts:49`, `groq-llm.ts:58` and `openai-llm.ts:63` all do an unguarded `await response.json()` outside any try. Every attempt after the first billed attempt is a second full provider charge, but `post-process.ts` deducts only the single `llmResponse.costUsd` that the winning attempt reports.\n54\tFailure scenario: A truncated or non-JSON 200 body from Cerebras (the default provider, retried up to `retriesFor('groq') = 3` times) means the provider generated and billed tokens on attempt 1, `response.json()` throws, `retryWithBackoff` sleeps and calls again — up to 4 paid completions, one deduction. Worse, a JSON parse error carries no `.status`, so `shouldFallback()` (`lib/llm-provider.ts:176`) returns false and `post-process.ts:171` returns a 500 with **no deduction at all** — the user gets an error and pays nothing for up to 4 billed LLM completions.\n55\tConfidence: medium\n56\t\n57\t### Candidate\n58\tFile: `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/providers/anthropic.ts`\n59\tLine: 136\n60\tInvariant: I2\n61\tClaim: The streaming Anthropic `fetch` carries only `abortController.signal` (fired solely on client disconnect) and no `AbortSignal.timeout`, so a stalled upstream leaves `start()` suspended forever; `costPromise` is never resolved and the `costPromise.then(...)` deduction in `assistant.ts:350` never runs on any path.\n62\tFailure scenario: Anthropic accepts the request (billing the input tokens for the screenshot), begins the SSE stream, then stalls without closing the socket — a known failure mode during provider incidents. The client eventually times out on its own side without triggering `cancel()` on the server's `ReadableStream`, `resolveCost` is never called, and the request is billed by Anthropic but charged 0 credits. Under a provider incident this silently affects every concurrent `/assistant` request on the machine, and the pinned promises are also invisible to the SIGTERM drain.\n63\tConfidence: low\n64\t\n65\t### Coverage\n66\t**Examined buckets:**\n67\t- **B1 cloud-entry-and-auth** — `middleware/credits.ts` read end to end (the deduction primitive, `inFlightDeductions`, `drainPendingDeductions`); `index.ts` SIGTERM/`gracefulShutdown` drain path; `middleware/auth.ts` reviewed only for whether it can deduct (it cannot).\n68\t- **B2 cloud-routes** — all 5 route files read for every `return`/exit path: `transcribe.ts`, `post-process.ts`, `assistant.ts`, `ws-streaming-deepgram.ts`, `usage.ts`. `usage.ts` contains no deduction and is read-only w.r.t. billing.\n69\t- **B3 cloud-stt-providers** — all 11 STT adapters swept for `no_speech` returns and for post-200 `ProviderUnavailableError` throws (the two \"already billed, not charged\" idioms); `providers/utils.ts` `retryWithBackoff` read in full.\n70\t- **B4 cloud-llm-providers** — `anthropic.ts` read end to end (both `requestAnthropicChat` and `streamAnthropicChat`); `lib/llm-provider.ts` `callWithRetry`/`shouldFallback` read in full; `cerebras.ts`/`groq-llm.ts`/`openai-llm.ts` checked for post-200 throw sites.\n71\t- **B5 cloud-billing-and-google** — `cost-calculator.ts` `creditsForCost`/`estimateCreditsForCost` (the 0.1 floor that makes several `> 0` guards vacuous).\n72\t- **B6 next-money-api** — `app/api/license/credits/route.ts` GET+POST read in full; `deductCreditBalance` → `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance` in `src/lib/db-layer.ts`. The deduct POST is a single atomic ledger spend; no double-application path found on the Next.js side (Stripe **grant** dedupe is I5's territory, not reported here).\n73\t\n74\t**Not examined / partially examined:**\n75\t- **B7–B12** (next edge/auth, tRPC, db-and-services beyond `deductCreditBalance`, public site, account UI, next tests) — no `deductCredits`/deduction call sites exist there; the only money-write reachable from the Fly service is `POST /api/license/credits`, which was covered under B6. Deliberately skipped as out-of-invariant.\n76\t- `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance`'s internal transaction was read only at its entry point — I confirmed it is a single atomic call per HTTP POST but did not audit its grant-selection SQL for partial application under contention.\n77\t- `gcs-storage.ts` (google-chirp's GCS upload is a real but negligible paid operation that is never deducted on a failed transcription) — noted, not filed, as the cost is sub-cent.\n78\t\n79\t**Leads refuted:**\n80\t- **`assistant.ts:350` — \"a rejected `costPromise` drops the charge entirely\": REFUTED.** `costPromise` is built at `anthropic.ts:119` as `new Promise((resolve) => { resolveCost = resolve })` — the executor never captures `reject`, so the promise is structurally incapable of rejecting. Every branch of `start()` (`!response.ok` → 163, no body → 171, success → 229, catch → 247) and `cancel()` (→ 258) calls `resolveCost`. The one `return` without a resolve (line 233) is correct: it is reached only when `signal.aborted`, and only `cancel()` aborts, having already resolved synchronously on the preceding line. The real defects on this path are different: the `costUsd > 0` gate dropping a pre-`message_start` disconnect, and the un-timed-out `fetch` leaving the promise unsettled (filed above).\n81\t- **`ws-streaming-deepgram.ts` `endSession()` concurrently reachable from `onClose`, `onError` and Deepgram's `close` listener → double deduction: REFUTED.** Although `endSession` is `async` and all three call sites `await` it, the guard `if (sessionEnded) return; sessionEnded = true;` (lines 189-190) executes synchronously at the top of the function body before any `await`. On a single-threaded event loop no interleaving is possible between the read and the write, so at most one deduction can ever fire. The genuine defects on this route are under-billing/over-billing at the boundaries (0-second sessions charged 0.1; whole sessions lost on SIGTERM), not duplication.\n82\t- **`performDeduction` bypassing `deductCredits()`: REFUTED.** `grep performDeduction` across both services returns exactly two hits, both in `middleware/credits.ts` (the declaration at line 132 and the single call at line 124 inside `deductCredits`). No call site escapes `inFlightDeductions`. However, the drain it feeds is undermined by the separate `recordLicenseUsage` swallow bug filed above — draining a promise that resolves without having charged anything provides no protection.\n83\t- **`post-process.ts` double deduction (catch-path at 256 + happy-path at 280): REFUTED.** The `catch (extractError)` block returns a 500 immediately after deducting, so the two sites are mutually exclusive. The deduct POST is a single atomic ledger spend; no double-application path found on the Next.js side (Stripe **grant** dedupe is I5's territory, not reported here).\n\n**Not examined / partially examined:**\n- **B7–B12** (next edge/auth, tRPC, db-and-services beyond `deductCreditBalance`, public site, account UI, next tests) — no `deductCredits`/deduction call sites exist there; the only money-write reachable from the Fly service is `POST /api/license/credits`, which was covered under B6. Deliberately skipped as out-of-invariant.\n- `spendCreditGrantsByProvenance`'s internal transaction was read only at its entry point — I confirmed it is a single atomic call per HTTP POST but did not audit its grant-selection SQL for partial application under contention.\n- `gcs-storage.ts` (google-chirp's GCS upload is a real but negligible paid operation that is never deducted on a failed transcription) — noted, not filed, as the cost is sub-cent.\n\n**Leads refuted:**\n- **`assistant.ts:350` — \"a rejected `costPromise` drops the charge entirely\": REFUTED.** `costPromise` is built at `anthropic.ts:119` as `new Promise((resolve) => { resolveCost = resolve })` — the executor never captures `reject`, so the promise is structurally incapable of rejecting. Every branch of `start()` (`!response.ok` → 163, no body → 171, success → 229, catch → 247) and `cancel()` (→ 258) calls `resolveCost`. The one `return` without a resolve (line 233) is correct: it is reached only when `signal.aborted`, and only `cancel()` aborts, having already resolved synchronously on the preceding line. The real defects on this path are different: the `costUsd > 0` gate dropping a pre-`message_start` disconnect, and the un-timed-out `fetch` leaving the promise unsettled (filed above).\n- **`ws-streaming-deepgram.ts` `endSession()` concurrently reachable from `onClose`, `onError` and Deepgram's `close` listener → double deduction: REFUTED.** Although `endSession` is `async` and all three call sites `await` it, the guard `if (sessionEnded) return; sessionEnded = true;` (lines 189-190) executes synchronously at the top of the function body before any `await`. On a single-threaded event loop no interleaving is possible between the read and the write, so at most one deduction can ever fire. The genuine defects on this route are under-billing/over-billing at the boundaries (0-second sessions charged 0.1; whole sessions lost on SIGTERM), not duplication.\n- **`performDeduction` bypassing `deductCredits()`: REFUTED.** `grep performDeduction` across both services returns exactly two hits, both in `middleware/credits.ts` (the declaration at line 132 and the single call at line 124 inside `deductCredits`). No call site escapes `inFlightDeductions`. However, the drain it feeds is undermined by the separate `recordLicenseUsage` swallow bug filed above — draining a promise that resolves without having charged anything provides no protection.\n- **`post-process.ts` double deduction (catch-path at 256 + happy-path at 280): REFUTED.** The `catch (extractError)` block returns a 500 immediately after deducting, so the two sites are mutually exclusive. Separately, the code comment at lines 208-210 claiming the outer catch would bill a retry whose evaluation throws is *inaccurate* — `evaluateCompletionResponse(retryResponse.raw, text)` at line 215 sits inside the inner `try` and would be caught at line 234 — but the outcome is still exactly one deduction at line 280 including the retry cost, so the invariant holds despite the misleading comment.\n","numLines":84,"startLine":1,"totalLines":84}},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"db7a2d9b-d43d-4926-9188-47ab9d1f2e6b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/Users/ray/Desktop/ray-os","sessionId":"0ad0c358-bd4c-4ff8-872f-2f81b1b9e66c","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"main","slug":"lively-giggling-hammock"}

{"parentUuid":"b8415472-78cf-47aa-a8fe-eeb0aedf7a05","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"872714b7-1488-4cc4-8bad-5341f8c86fd3","agentId":"a5d34dd62f848ca0a","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KVMkmK1ZLCDyVJnsRZB9rv","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t## Stack & surfaces\n2\t\n3\tTwo independently deployed TypeScript services. **`nextjs/`** is a Next.js 15.5 App Router app on Vercel (React 18, next-intl v4 with 40 locales, HeroUI/Tailwind, Drizzle ORM over `node-postgres`, Better Auth with a magic-link plugin plus a custom `license-key` sign-in plugin, tRPC v10 with superjson, Stripe v20 + a legacy Polar SDK path, Resend for email, Upstash Redis for rate limiting). It is the **system of record for money**: license keys (\"Account Keys\"), the `credit_grants` ledger, the `credit_balances` denormalized cache, and `stripe_processed_events` idempotency table all live here, exposed to the outside world through 23 REST route handlers plus one tRPC catch-all. **`hyperwhisper-cloud/`** is a Bun/Hono service on Fly.io that proxies audio to 11 STT providers and 7 LLM providers; it holds **no database** — it authenticates by POSTing the license key to `nextjs`'s `/api/license/validate`, caches the `{isValid, credits}` verdict in Upstash Redis for 1h (`license:<key>`), and charges usage by POSTing back to `/api/license/credits`. Trust boundaries: the Fly service trusts only the TCP peer address (`fdaa::/16` ⇒ off-edge ⇒ IP is `unknown`); the Next.js service trusts the Stripe signature, an `x-internal-secret` header, a Better Auth session cookie, and — critically — **the bare license key as a bearer credential** for all `/api/license/*` and `/api/account/*` endpoints. Shared mutable state: `inFlightDeductions: Set<Promise>` (cloud, drained on SIGTERM), the `_jwtClient`/`_inflight` Google token singletons, the ~1h in-memory model-list cache in `nextjs/lib/services/model-list.ts`, and the Redis license cache which both services write. Concurrency points: fire-and-forget `deductCredits()` after response flush, `costPromise.then(...)` after an SSE stream, the Deepgram WebSocket bridge, Stripe webhook re-deliveries, and `db.transaction` blocks in `db-layer.ts`.\n4\t\n5\t## Entry points\n6\t\n7\t**hyperwhisper-cloud (Hono, `src/index.ts`)**\n8\t- `GET /health`, `GET /warmup` — unauthenticated, no input.\n9\t- `POST /transcribe` (`src/routes/transcribe.ts`) — raw `audio/*` body; `X-STT-Provider`/`X-STT-Model`/`X-STT-Domain` headers; `account_key`|`license_key`, `language`, `initial_prompt`, `mode` query params read via `rawQuery()`. Buffers up to 2 GB into RAM.\n10\t- `POST /post-process` (`src/routes/post-process.ts`) — JSON `{text ≤100k chars, prompt, account_key|license_key}`; `X-LLM-Provider`/model headers.\n11\t- `POST /assistant` (`src/routes/assistant.ts`) — `multipart/form-data`: `account_key`, `messages` (JSON, ≤6 MB, may contain inline base64 images), `prompt`, `image` file (≤10 MB). Returns an SSE stream from Anthropic.\n12\t- `GET /usage` (`src/routes/usage.ts`) — `account_key`|`license_key`|`identifier`, `force_refresh` query params.\n13\t- `GET /ws/streaming-deepgram` (`src/routes/ws-streaming-deepgram.ts`) — WebSocket upgrade; `account_key`, `language`, `vocabulary` query params; binary audio frames proxied to `wss://api.deepgram.com`.\n14\t- `SIGTERM`/`SIGINT` handler — drains `inFlightDeductions` for 4 s.\n15\t- `hyperwhisper-cloud/scripts/smoke-test.ts` — operator-run, hits the live service.\n16\t\n17\t**nextjs — public REST (all unauthenticated unless noted)**\n18\t- `POST /api/license/validate` + alias `/api/account/validate` — `{license_key, include_credits, device_id, device_name}`. IP rate-limited 30/min. Falls back to a live Polar API call and can **mint a license + 5000 credits** on a DB miss.\n19\t- `POST /api/license/activate` + `/api/account/activate` — same shape, rate-limited, legacy.\n20\t- `POST /api/license/deactivate` + `/api/account/deactivate` — stub, always 200.\n21\t- `GET /api/license/credits` + `/api/account/credits` — `?license_key=`; returns balance. **No rate limit.**\n22\t- `POST /api/license/credits` + `/api/account/credits` — `{license_key, amount, metadata}`; **atomically deducts `amount` credits. No rate limit, no auth beyond the key.** This is the sole write path used by the Fly service.\n23\t- `POST /api/checkout/credits` — `{amount: 5..500, licenseKey?, email?}`; creates Stripe customers and Checkout Sessions. **No auth, no rate limit.**\n24\t- `GET /api/checkout` — redirect to `/credits`.\n25\t- `POST /api/webhooks/stripe` — Stripe signature verified; handles `checkout.session.completed`, `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded`, `checkout.session.async_payment_failed`, `charge.refunded`.\n26\t- `POST /api/webhooks/add-blog-post` — Outrank bearer token (+ optional HMAC); upserts `blog_posts` and calls `revalidatePath`.\n27\t- `GET /api/download` — `?platform=&arch=`; fetches an appcast XML and builds a dynamic `RegExp` from `arch`.\n28\t- `GET /api/config`, `GET /api/internal/models`, `GET /models` — cached public JSON; `/models` and `/api/internal/models` fan out to 6 paid provider list APIs on a cache miss.\n29\t- `GET|POST /api/auth/[...all]` — Better Auth (magic link, `/sign-in/license-key` at 5/min).\n30\t- `GET|POST /api/trpc/[trpc]` — `download.*` (public), `customer.*` (protected), `admin.*` (admin).\n31\t- `GET /api/customer/profile` — Better Auth session required.\n32\t- `GET /[locale]/user/auth/sign-out` — route handler.\n33\t\n34\t**nextjs — internal REST (`x-internal-secret` header, timing-safe)**\n35\t- `POST /api/internal/grant-license` — mints a key + credits for an email.\n36\t- `POST /api/internal/granted-emails` — bulk email dump.\n37\t- `POST /api/internal/licenses-for-email` — keys + balances for an email.\n38\t\n39\t**nextjs — middleware & rendering**\n40\t- `middleware.ts` — matcher `/((?!api|models(?:/|$)|_next|_vercel|docs|.*\\..*).*)`. Gates `/{locale}/user/customers` with a real `fetch('/api/auth/get-session')` + `role === \"admin\"`; gates all other `/{locale}/user/*` on **cookie presence only**; runs `next-intl` for everything else.\n41\t- 22 `page.tsx` files under `app/[locale]/`, plus `sitemap.ts` / `robots.ts`.\n42\t\n43\t## Invariants (ranked)\n44\t\n45\t### I1: Every credit reservation taken at preflight is ≥ the total cost actually deducted for that request, across all retries, fallbacks and re-prompts.\n46\tWhy it matters: the reservation is the only thing standing between a low-balance user and unbounded provider spend the business eats; under-reserving turns each request into a free-money bug at HyperWhisper's expense.\n47\tSearch strategy: `grep -n \"ESTIMATED_POST_PROCESS_CREDITS\\|ESTIMATED_ASSISTANT_CREDITS\\|estimateCreditsForProviderFallbacks\\|minimumStreamingCredits\"` — then trace each. `post-process.ts` reserves a **flat `1.0`** yet can spend `retriesFor(provider)` primary attempts (up to 3 for groq) + a fallback provider's full retry budget + a *prompt-leakage retry on a third provider* whose cost is `costUsd += retryResponse.costUsd`. `assistant.ts` reserves `3.0 × countInlineImages()` but the multipart `image` file adds an image `countInlineImages` never counts, and output tokens are unbounded. In `transcribe.ts`, cross-check `FALLBACK_CHAINS` against the `rates` array built in `estimateCreditsForProviderFallbacks` — grep `costUsd +=`, `retriesFor(`, `callWithRetry(`.\n48\t\n49\t### I2: Every upstream call that has already cost money results in exactly one `deductCredits()`, on every exit path — including thrown-exception and stream-error paths — and never more than one.\n50\tWhy it matters: a missed deduction is silent revenue loss at scale; a duplicated one double-charges a paying customer.\n51\tSearch strategy: in `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/`, grep `deductCredits(` (7 call sites) and `return c.json\\|return errorResponse\\|return new Response`; for each `return` that follows a successful provider call, confirm a deduction precedes it. Specific suspects: `assistant.ts:350` — `costPromise.then(...)` bills only on resolve, so a rejected `costPromise` (Anthropic stream aborted mid-flight after tokens were billed) drops the charge entirely; `ws-streaming-deepgram.ts` `endSession()` is guarded by `sessionEnded` but is reachable from `onClose`, `onError`, and the Deepgram `close` listener concurrently. Also grep `performDeduction` — any call site bypassing `deductCredits()` escapes `inFlightDeductions` and is lost on SIGTERM.\n52\t\n53\t### I3: A `license:<key>` Redis entry with `isValid:false` is written only for a *definitive* verdict (HTTP 4xx from the licensing API); transient failures (5xx, timeout, network, unparseable body) must leave the cache untouched.\n54\tWhy it matters: `LICENSE_CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 3600` — one Vercel cold start or a malformed body during a deploy locks a paying customer out of transcription for a full hour across every Fly region.\n55\tSearch strategy: `grep -rn \"cacheLicense(\" hyperwhisper-cloud/src`. `middleware/auth.ts:70-79` implements the rule correctly (explicit `if (response.status >= 500) return ... ; // not caching`). `routes/usage.ts:44-52` (`validateLicenseAndGetCredits`) does **not**: it computes `isValid = data.valid === true` after `response.json().catch(() => ({}))` and unconditionally calls `cacheLicense` — so a 500, an HTML error page, or an empty body all poison the cache with `isValid:false`. Verify every `cacheLicense` call is preceded by a status check.\n56\t\n57\t### I4: Every STT adapter that returns a non-empty transcript returns a strictly positive `durationSeconds` (falling back to `estimateSecondsFromBytes` when the upstream duration field is absent or zero).\n58\tWhy it matters: `costUsd` is `durationSeconds × rate` for duration-billed providers, so a missing field means a real, provider-billed transcription is charged **0 credits** — a free-transcription oracle triggered by any upstream response-shape change.\n59\tSearch strategy: `grep -ln \"estimateSecondsFromBytes\" hyperwhisper-cloud/src/providers/*.ts` and diff against the 11 STT adapters. Present in `assemblyai.ts`, `mistral.ts`, `soniox.ts`, `openai.ts`, and `google-chirp.ts` (via its own `estimateAudioSeconds`); **absent in `deepgram.ts`, `groq.ts`, `elevenlabs.ts`, `xai-stt.ts`, `azure-mai.ts`**. For each, read where `durationSeconds` is derived (`data.metadata.duration`, `data.duration`, last word `.end`, `data.durationMilliseconds`) and confirm a guard before `compute*TranscriptionCost(...)`.\n60\t\n61\t### I5: Every Stripe webhook handler that mutates licenses or credits dedupes on a stable Stripe id before mutating, and returns non-2xx (so Stripe retries) on any *transient* failure.\n62\tWhy it matters: Stripe delivers at-least-once and fires both `checkout.session.completed` and `checkout.session.async_payment_succeeded` for the same purchase; a missing guard double-grants credits, and swallowing an error returns 200 and permanently loses the grant.\n63\tSearch strategy: in `nextjs/lib/services/stripe-webhook.ts`, grep `grantCreditLot(\\|grantCreditsForStripeEvent(\\|refundCreditGrant(\\|updateAccountKey(`. `handleCreditPurchase` correctly routes through `grantCreditsForStripeEvent` (keyed on `stripe_processed_events.stripe_object_id`); `handleLicensePurchase:126` calls the **un-deduped `grantCreditLot({sourceType:\"license_bundle\", sourceId: session.id})`** and its only guard is a prior `findAccountByStripeSession` read — check whether `grantCreditLotInTransaction`'s `onConflictDoNothing` target actually covers `(sourceType, sourceId)`. Also `app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:121-130`: `handleChargeRefunded` errors are caught and the route still returns `{received:true}` — a refund clawback lost to a transient DB blip is never retried.\n64\t\n65\t### I6: No credit amount, price, or quantity that determines what a user is charged or granted is taken from client-controlled input without server-side re-derivation or a hard bound.\n66\tWhy it matters: this is the direct path to minting free credits or draining another key's wallet.\n67\tSearch strategy: `grep -rn \"body.amount\\|input.amount\\|metadata.credit_amount\\|creditAmount\" nextjs/app/api nextjs/lib nextjs/server`. `POST /api/license/credits` accepts `amount` straight off the wire (bounded only by `validateCreditDeductionAmount`: finite, >0, ≤1,000,000, 2 dp) and deducts it — with the license key as the sole credential and no rate limit, so anyone holding a leaked key can zero a balance in one request. `session.metadata.credit_amount` is trusted in `handleCreditPurchase`; confirm nothing but `/api/checkout/credits` can write that metadata. Confirm `computeCreditPurchase()` is the only source of `creditCents`/`feeCents` and that `components/credits/CreditsPurchase.tsx:92` sends only `amount`, never a price.\n68\t\n69\t### I7: Every unauthenticated Next.js route handler whose work fans out to a paid third party or writes a durable object is rate-limited.\n70\tWhy it matters: one cheap POST maps 1:1 to a Polar call, a Stripe customer/session creation, or a 6-provider model fan-out — free amplification and quota/cost burn against an open endpoint.\n71\tSearch strategy: `grep -rLn \"RateLimiter\\|\\.limit(\" nextjs/app/api/**/route.ts` and compare against `nextjs/lib/rate-limit.ts` (only `downloadEmailRateLimiter` and `licenseValidateRateLimiter` exist). `/api/license/validate` and `/api/license/activate` are covered; `/api/license/credits` (GET **and** the deducting POST), `/api/checkout/credits` (creates real `stripe.customers` + Checkout Sessions), `/api/download` (outbound appcast fetch + dynamic RegExp), and `/api/internal/models` are not. For `/api/internal/models` and `/models`, verify the `s-maxage=3600` header plus `fetchAvailableModels`'s in-memory TTL actually bound the fan-out on a cache-busted or cold-lambda request.\n72\t\n73\t### I8: Any handler returning or mutating a specific user's data derives that user from the Better Auth session (or an admin check), never from a client-supplied id; middleware cookie-presence is never the only gate.\n74\tWhy it matters: `middleware.ts` checks only that `better-auth.session_token` **exists** for `/{locale}/user/*` — a forged or expired cookie value passes it. If a page or procedure then trusts that, an unauthenticated caller reads customer data.\n75\tSearch strategy: for each of `app/[locale]/user/(authenticated)/{dashboard,devices,customers}/page.tsx` and `layout.tsx`, grep for `auth.api.getSession`; any page that renders account data without one relies on the presence-only check. Cross-check the tRPC routers: `grep -n \"publicProcedure\\|protectedProcedure\\|adminProcedure\" nextjs/server/api/routers/**/*.ts` — every `admin/*` procedure must use `adminProcedure` (`ctx.isAdmin` from `user.role === \"admin\"`), and every `customer.*` procedure must key off `ctx.user.id`/`ctx.user.email`, never an `input`-supplied id. Also verify `src/db/schema/auth.ts` marks `role` as `input: false` so it cannot be set at sign-up.\n76\t\n77\t### I9: Every comparison of a caller-supplied credential against an env secret is timing-safe **and** fails closed when the env var is unset or empty.\n78\tWhy it matters: `timingSafeEqualSecret(received, expected)` returns `false` when `expected` is falsy — good — but any `===` comparison, or a route that skips the check when the env var is missing, silently opens an admin/mint endpoint in a misconfigured environment.\n79\tSearch strategy: `grep -rn \"process.env\\..*SECRET\\|process.env\\..*TOKEN\\|process.env\\..*KEY\" nextjs/app/api nextjs/lib | grep -n \"===\\|!==\\|==\"`. Confirm all three `/api/internal/*` routes call `timingSafeEqualSecret(secret, process.env.HYPERWHISPER_INTERNAL_SECRET)` before any DB work (they do at line ~11 of each). In `app/api/webhooks/add-blog-post/route.ts`, check the interaction between `OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_SIGNING_SECRET` and `OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_TOKEN`: when signing is enabled, verify the bearer path cannot be used as an alternative bypass, and that a missing `OUTRANK_WEBHOOK_TOKEN` doesn't make `timingSafeEqualSecret` the only barrier to a mint-shaped mutation. Also `app/api/webhooks/stripe/route.ts:33-40` correctly 500s on a missing `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` — confirm no sibling handler does the opposite.\n80\t\n81\t### I10: Every `fetch()` to a third-party API carries an `AbortSignal.timeout` or an `AbortController`.\n82\tWhy it matters: a hung upstream leaves an unresolved promise pinning the request scope — on a 1 GB Fly machine that bloats the Bun worker until OOM-kill, and on Vercel it burns the full function max-duration.\n83\tSearch strategy: `grep -rn \"fetch(\" hyperwhisper-cloud/src nextjs/lib nextjs/app | grep -v \"signal:\"`. In the cloud service all 10 STT adapters route through `fetchWithTimeout()` in `providers/utils.ts` (default `DEFAULT_PROVIDER_TIMEOUT_MS = 15_000`), but **all 7 LLM provider files — `anthropic.ts`, `cerebras.ts`, `groq-llm.ts`, `gemini-llm.ts`, `mistral-llm.ts`, `openai-llm.ts`, `xai-llm.ts` — use bare `fetch()` with no timeout** (`streamAnthropicChat`'s AbortController fires only on client disconnect, not on a stall). Note `gcs-storage.ts` maintains a *second, duplicated* `fetchWithTimeoutMs` implementation that can drift from `providers/utils.ts`. On the Next.js side check `lib/services/model-list.ts` `apiGet()` and the Polar SDK calls in `src/lib/license-validation.ts` (no timeout is configurable there, yet it sits behind an unauthenticated endpoint).\n84\t\n85\t### I11: In the WebSocket bridge, every acquired resource is released on every path, and closing one side always closes the other.\n86\tWhy it matters: an orphaned upstream Deepgram socket keeps metering billable audio and holds a Fly file descriptor; a leaked `setInterval` survives the request.\n87\tSearch strategy: read `hyperwhisper-cloud/src/routes/ws-streaming-deepgram.ts` end to end and check each early `return`. The `MAX_SESSION_AUDIO_BYTES` breach at line 308 sends an error and calls `clientSocket.close(1009)` but **never calls `closeUpstream()` / `deepgramWs.close()`** — verify whether the resulting client close reliably fires `onClose`. Also: `pingInterval` is cleared only inside `endSession()`; the `apiKey`-missing early return at line 238 sets it never, but confirm no path sets the interval then exits without `endSession()`. Grep `setInterval(\\|clearInterval(\\|getReader()\\|releaseLock()\\|\\.cancel(` across `src/` — `providers/anthropic.ts`'s `streamAnthropicChat` `start()` catch block never calls `reader.cancel()` when the failure is not a client abort.\n88\t\n89\t### I12: The provider and model reported back to the client (`X-STT-Provider`, `X-STT-Model`, deduction `metadata`) always names the provider/model that actually ran and was priced.\n90\tWhy it matters: `transcribe.ts:669` does `result.source as Provider` and looks the price up from that; if an adapter returns a `source` that isn't its own registry id, or reports a `model` it didn't run, the charge is computed against the wrong rate table and support has no audit trail.\n91\tSearch strategy: `grep -n \"source:\" hyperwhisper-cloud/src/providers/*.ts` and check each returned literal against `TranscriptionSource` in `providers/types.ts` **and** against `SttProviderId` in `lib/stt-models.ts` — these are two parallel unions that must stay in sync (`TranscriptionSource` additionally carries `'no_speech'`). Then confirm `usedModel = result.model || attemptModel` (line 511) is what reaches both the `X-STT-Model` header and `stt_model` in the `deductCredits` metadata, and that `getProviderDef(current).defaultModel` on a fallback hop matches the rate actually used in `estimateCreditsForProviderFallbacks`. Also check `cost-calculator.ts`'s `rates[model] ?? rates[defaultModel]` fallback — an unknown model id is silently re-priced at the default rate.\n92\t\n93\t### I13: Every value crossing a trust boundary is validated (or allow-listed/escaped) before it is used to index, interpolate, allocate, or render.\n94\tWhy it matters: these are the crash-and-injection sinks — an unvalidated `req.json()` destructure 500s, an unescaped name lands script in a transactional email from a trusted sender, an unbounded `Content-Length` allocates memory before auth.\n95\tSearch strategy: (a) `grep -rn \"await req.json()\\|await request.json()\\|c.req.json()\" nextjs/app/api hyperwhisper-cloud/src` and confirm each result is shape-checked before destructuring — `app/api/license/credits/route.ts:82` does `const body = await req.json(); const { license_key, amount, metadata } = body;` with no null guard and an entirely unvalidated `metadata` that is then logged. (b) `grep -rn \"escapeHtml\" nextjs/lib/templates/` — all four templates escape `customerName`; verify every *other* interpolated field in the 17/15/21/14 `${...}` sites is either a number or system-generated. (c) `grep -rn \"dangerouslySetInnerHTML\" nextjs` — `app/[locale]/blog/[slug]/page.tsx:170` renders `post.html` raw from the Outrank webhook. (d) `grep -rn \"new RegExp(\" nextjs` — `app/api/download/route.ts:66` builds a RegExp from `arch`, guarded by `parseWindowsArch`'s allow-list; confirm no sibling does the same without one. (e) Confirm `Content-Length` is parsed and bounded *before* `arrayBuffer()`/`formData()` on every cloud route.\n96\t\n97\t### I14: No user-facing path hardcodes a single locale, and every message key referenced by a component exists in all 40 locale files.\n98\tWhy it matters: `localePrefix: \"always\"` over 40 locales means a hardcoded `/en/...` silently strips 39 languages from a cache invalidation or a post-auth redirect, and a missing key makes `next-intl` throw at render — a hard 500 on that locale only, invisible from an English-language smoke test.\n99\tSearch strategy: `grep -rn '\"/en/\\|`/en/\\|/en/' nextjs/app nextjs/src nextjs/lib` — `app/api/webhooks/add-blog-post/route.ts:386,403` calls `revalidatePath(\"/en/blog/...\")` and `revalidatePath(\"/en/blog\")` only, so 39 locales keep serving stale blog indexes; `src/lib/license-key-redirect.ts:1` pins `DEFAULT_LICENSE_KEY_REDIRECT = \"/en/user/dashboard\"`. Then diff key sets: `for f in nextjs/messages/*.json; do echo \"$f $(python3 -c 'import json,sys;d=json.load(open(sys.argv[1]));print(len(json.dumps(d)))' $f)\"; done` — better, flatten and set-diff each of the 40 files against `en.json`. Finally, `grep -rLn \"setRequestLocale\" nextjs/app/\\[locale\\]/**/page.tsx` — only `app/[locale]/layout.tsx` calls it today, so verify against `routing.ts` whether any statically-rendered page under `[locale]` needs it to avoid bailing to dynamic rendering or leaking the wrong locale.\n100\t\n101\t## Partition\n102\t\n103\t*(verified programmatically: all 210 files in `bug-hunt/scope-files.txt` `nextjs/src/content/blog.ts`\n163\t\n164\t### B11: next-account-ui — 29 files\n165\t- `nextjs/app/[locale]/user/**` (incl. `(authenticated)/` dashboard, devices, customers; sign-in;
